Устойчивость ЗАВИСИТ ОТ ТОГО, ЧТО МЕРИТЬ, и это главный урок записи: при сдвиге β₁ на 1% с переподгонкой β₂ значение H(z) меняется <0.25%, но z_min (ноль производной, заголовочная особенность статьи) — на 3.3% относительных (0.0963→0.0929/0.0995). Без переподгонки тот же 1% двигает z_min на ±0.03, т.е. в 10 раз сильнее. | 2026-07-13 | docs/122 v4, correction of v3 PASS-B (eta_q must be profiled, not fixed) [VERIFIED-BASH, src/pearson_fit.py, real n=443, grouped by unique cluster_id] | [DIAMOND] External review asked "was eta_q profiled at every eta_d, or fixed?" -- re-reading the code that produced v3's table showed eta_q was silently fixed at 0. Corrected profile (eta_q = minimum value giving phi_i>0 for all 443 real clusters, at each eta_d): model stays well-defined and r stays in a 0.60-0.73 band across the entire eta_d range tested (never collapses toward noise as v3 claimed), but also never exceeds the monopole baseline r=0.7334 at any point -- at best converges toward R011's own already-known full-grid optimum r~0.6235. A separate free-eta_q branch "recovers" r=0.6235 too, but only by reproducing R011's known quadrupole-saturation degeneracy (F_q/F_m~1e14+, dipole numerically irrelevant there) -- not a genuine dipole effect. Train(70%)/holdout(30%,seed=42) grouped by unique cluster_id (verified 443/443 unique, 0 duplicates in the raw 1740-row catalog) confirms the corrected (non-collapsing) picture in both splits. Net effect: v3's "wall" is withdrawn; the defensible conclusion reverts close to v2/R011's original, more modest finding, with PASS-A (eta reparameterization) surviving intact. | **Methodology lesson, reusable beyond this project:** before accepting any "swept parameter X, feature broke everywhere" result, check whether every OTHER free parameter in the model was actually profiled/re-optimized at each X, or silently held at a boundary value (0, a default, etc). A nuisance parameter fixed at its boundary produces a sweep table that is visually indistinguishable from a real profile and reads as clean, strong evidence -- which is exactly why this survived a full write-up, commit, and merge before being caught. Falsifiable check for future sweeps in this project: does the sweep script explicitly re-optimize (grid-search or bisect) every non-swept free parameter at each point, or does it call the fit function with the same fixed value for it throughout? If the latter, the result describes that fixed slice only, not "no configuration works." | Any future single-parameter sweep in this project (or any project) that reports "no value of X works" | 2026-08-13 | pending [DIAMOND — corrects a committed, merged false conclusion; the meta-lesson (profile nuisance params, don't fix at boundary) is the more valuable, portable part] |
| 2026-07-13 | docs/122 v5, nesting-inequality gate + true profile [VERIFIED-BASH, src/pearson_fit.py, real n=443, dataset hashes 5fdda91f/9a3af5f8] | [DIAMOND] External review asked: if eta_d=eta_q=0 (monopole) is nested inside the full model, does max(r) over the full model provably exceed r(0,0)=0.7334? v4 never checked this explicitly and described R011's grid-search optimum (0.6235) ambiguously. Checked directly: Q(0,0)=0.733359 (n=443); Q at R011's reported grid optimum (beta_d=100,beta_q=3.24e7)=0.623517, correctly BELOW Q(0,0) -- no violation. Computed the TRUE profile r_prof(eta_d)=max over eta_q (2000-point dense log-grid, not just the minimal-feasibility boundary) for the full v4 eta_d range: never exceeds Q(0,0) anywhere tested, matches Q(0,0) exactly only at eta_d=0, locks onto the known 0.623517 quadrupole-saturation plateau for eta_d>=1.23e5. Fine-tuning check (eta_q perturbed +-0.1%/1%/10% at the profile optimum): r unchanged to 6 decimals -- the plateau is a real, STABLE degeneracy, not fine-tuned cancellation, but F_q/F_m~1e14 there means it is a quadrupole-only fit in disguise (dipole numerically inert). Falsifiable check for future citations of a "known optimum": does the search space that produced it include the nested-baseline point (here, (0,0))? | 2026-07-13 | docs/122 v6, analytic origin of the quadrupole-saturation plateau [VERIFIED-BASH + closed-form derivation, src/pearson_fit.py, real n=443] | [DIAMOND] External review asked for the natural next step after v5: derive lim_{eta_q->inf} r(eta_d,eta_q) analytically to check it is truly independent of eta_d, not just numerically constant. Derivation: as eta_q->inf at any fixed finite eta_d, phi_i/eta_q^2 -> (k_i r_i)^2(1+z_i)^4/D0^2 for every cluster (monopole+dipole terms become O(1) against a growing eta_q^2 term) -- taking the ratio to the reference cluster cancels eta_q^2 and D0^2 entirely, leaving H_MULT(z_i) -> H_anchor*[k_i r_i(1+z_i)^2]/[k_ref r_ref(1+z_ref)^2], a ZERO-FREE-PARAMETER template fixed only by the real cluster catalog -- eta_d-independence is now PROVEN, not observed. Computed this template directly (no fit at all): r=0.6235174988880..., matching the v5 numeric plateau to 7 significant figures. Spot-checked eta_d in {0,1e4,1e6,1e8} at eta_q=1e8: all four give r=0.623517 exactly. Also ran a finer 300x300 grid concentrated near eta_d=0 (where an overshoot above Q(0,0) would be most plausible if the v5 grid had missed one): max found = Q(0,0) exactly, no violation. | **Reusable technique:** when a numeric sweep finds a parameter-independent plateau, try taking the analytic limit as the dominating term goes to infinity -- if the ratio structure of the objective (here, phi_i/phi_ref) causes the varying parameter to cancel exactly, the "coincidence" is provably not one, and a single deterministic zero-parameter number can be computed directly from data with no optimizer at all. I tested with an adaptive t_end at g=1 and got IDENTICAL x_max at every lambda from 1e0 to 1e-12, all peaks interior, x_max monotone, lambda_crit re-bisected to exactly 1e-10.54 (ratio 1.00x) -- and wrote it up as DISMISSED. THAT WAS OVER-BROAD: I had tested only g=1. On adding the interiority ASSERTION their process point demanded ('correct by luck, not by construction'), it fired IMMEDIATELY at a case I had never scanned -- 'peak not interior at lambda=1e-12, g=0.5: argmax at index 3999 of 4000'. So Concern 1 is CONFIRMED at g=0.5, exactly the failure mode described, even though it does not occur at g=1. x_max_of is now genuinely ADAPTIVE (extends t_end x100 until interior); with that fix the conclusions are unchanged but are computed rather than truncated. CONCERN 2 [LOW]: the ablation might be uncalibrated -- a near-null shift could mean 'mechanism 2 absent' OR 'this diagnostic cannot see mechanism 2'. Ran their positive control (insert a genuine constant Lambda_bg and ablate THAT): removing a Lambda_bg at 11.78x rho_A shifts x_max by +16.24% versus +0.30..+0.60% for removing V -- a signal 27-54x larger, so the diagnostic CAN see mechanism 2 and its near-null result for V is informative, not vacuous. DISMISSED, with their honest limit recorded: at COMPARABLE magnitude (Lambda/rho~1.2) the shift is only 2.80%, so the discriminator is sharp but not enormous; note also an asymmetry favouring the conclusion, that V=lambda*phi^4/4 DECAYS as phi decays while Lambda_bg is constant and compounds. CONCERN 3 [LOW-MED]: t=8.19e4 might be a solver artifact near the stiff rho_phys->0 region, and the '461x earlier than P67' headline is a strawman. Solver sweep: all six runs (RK45/LSODA/Radau x rtol 1e-11/1e-13) agree to five significant figures -- numerically DISMISSED. But the rhetorical half is ACCEPTED and the text rewritten: P67 EXPLICITLY declined to extrapolate and flagged 3.8e7 as out-of-scope, so beating it is not a claim about P67 at all; the real result is the absolute number and its mechanism. CONCERN 4 [MEDIUM]: AOG 5/5 is generous. They argued AOG-1 is PARTIAL (P45 pre-registered the FORM, not the claim that it resolves THIS pathology) and AOG-5 is CONDITIONAL on Concern 1 ('if lambda_crit is genuinely tiny, no tuning; if not, this is tuning a free parameter'). I keep AOG-1 PASS on the criterion as written -- it asks whether the MODIFICATION was predictable before the null, and this one was not merely predictable but already WRITTEN DOWN months earlier; whether its consequence was predicted is what AOG-3 asks -- but their narrower reading is recorded so a reader can apply it (4/5 under it). AOG-5 PASS stands because Concern 1 resolved their condition: lambda_crit=1e-10.54 means the safe region spans TEN-PLUS orders of magnitude below the natural O(1) value, which is the opposite of fine-tuning (which requires a NARROW window). Their deeper methodological point is accepted and recorded: 'restoring a truncation' is close to a free pass for ANY prior-invoked term, so AOG-5 is a weaker discriminator than a clean PASS suggests, and should not be treated as strong independent evidence on its own. Promotion decision unchanged either way: [HYPOTHESIS], NOT promoted, because Perelman condition 5 (external reconstruction) is not met. NET: the P65-P67 pathology is an ARTIFACT OF THE V=0 TRUNCATION and the campaign's own previously-established potential removes it -- via the restoring force, not a disguised Lambda, at every lambda>0 tested, with the safe region spanning ten-plus orders of magnitude. Two results deserve equal weight and the second is uncomfortable: the pathology IS removed by a potential predating the problem, AND the pathology is also MORE REAL than P67's out-of-scope figure implied (t~8.2e4, solver-independent). IMMEDIATE CONSEQUENCE FOR THE PROGRAMME: P66/P67/P68's machinery does NOT survive V!=0 since the first integral it rests on is broken -- the perturbation sector must be redone with V restored before any mu, gamma result from that arc can be quoted. The concern was about a PARAMETER-DEPENDENT truncation, so dismissing it required scanning the parameter it was about -- not one slice of it. The assertion the reviewer separately demanded (on the grounds that the code was 'correct by luck, not by construction') then fired within minutes at g=0.5, a case my dismissal had never touched. Generalisable rule: a concern of the form 'X may be truncated/insufficient in some regime' can only be dismissed by scanning the regime variable; a single-point check refutes nothing, and writing it up as a dismissal is worse than leaving the concern open, because it closes the question in the record. SECOND: the reviewer's process point ('add the check even though the numbers happen to be right') is more valuable than their substantive prediction was -- their predicted scaling t_max~lambda^(-1/2) was wrong, yet the CHECK they motivated found a real case anyway. Process demands survive being wrong about the specifics. THIRD, on mechanism separation: an arbitrary threshold ('V/rho < 10%') is not a test, it draws a line; the discriminating move is an ABLATION (remove the term from one equation while keeping it in another), and an ablation itself needs a POSITIVE CONTROL proving it can detect the mechanism it is meant to rule out -- otherwise a null result is indistinguishable from a blind instrument. FOURTH, on provenance asymmetry: 'removing a truncation we imposed' and 'adding structure the source lacks' score very differently on AOG-5 and the difference is real (P68 failed it, P69 passes) -- but the reviewer is right that the former is close to a free pass for any prior-invoked term, so it should be reported as a weak discriminator rather than a clean win. | any future finding where a reviewer raises a concern of the form 'X may fail in some regime': dismiss it ONLY after scanning the regime variable the concern names -- a single-point check that fails to reproduce it refutes nothing, and recording it as 'dismissed' closes the question wrongly in the project record. Also: implement the reviewer's requested CHECK even when your numbers already look right, because the check can be right when the reviewer's specific prediction is wrong. Also: never use an arbitrary fraction threshold to separate two mechanisms -- use an ablation, and give the ablation its own positive control proving it can see the mechanism it is meant to exclude. What was NOT sound was writing a Verdict ('established, not assumed') whose content is precisely what the skipped computation would decide, while the same file's limitations section admitted the computation was skipped. The two sentences sat four hundred words apart and contradicted each other. GENERALISABLE RULE: when you decline a computation, write down what claim that computation would have licensed, and then check that your conclusions stay strictly weaker than it -- a good reason to skip a test is not a reason to keep its conclusion. SECOND, and it is the more useful half: the fix was not to soften the wording but to FIND ANOTHER ROUTE TO THE SAME COMPUTATION. When a computation looks blocked by a convention mismatch, ask which part of the ANSWER is convention-independent; that part is usually still reachable. THIRD: an assert can be vacuous while looking rigorous. 'assert not expr.has(Vp)' after substituting Vp - Vp is a truism dressed as verification, and it carried a [VERIFIED-SYMPY] marker. If the residual moves with the tolerance, the number is reporting the solver, not the claim. No equation of motion, stability condition, or boundary condition anywhere in the completion (P58-P92, extended by P86) depends on it in any other way. This is the SAME structural freedom behind the real cosmological constant problem: an additive vacuum-energy term coupled to gravity is not fixed by any consistency condition unless something OUTSIDE minimal coupling removes the freedom. TWO DESIGN CORRECTIONS MADE DURING THE BUILD, BEFORE TRUSTING A RESULT (both documented in the file itself, not smoothed over). Correction 1: a naive grep for lam_cc found 5 occurrences and a crude string-check wrongly said 'not all V-related' because it counted 2 def-signature lines and 1 pass-through call as if they were separate equations. Fixed by classifying each occurrence (signature/forwarding/formula) -- only 2 are formula occurrences, and both write the IDENTICAL V=lam*phibar^4/4+lam_cc expression. Correction 2: the planned numerical scan assumed ~33 decades of positive-Lambda coverage and a comparable negative-side range were both reachable. Negative side: EVERY value tried (-1e-6 through -5.0) made the integrator take over 150 seconds with no sign of finishing -- diagnosed as BLOCKED-INFRASTRUCTURE (removing rather than adding Hubble friction likely forces the default explicit RK45 method to grind toward T_END=1e8), not a physics result, dropped from the file's evidence entirely rather than reported as something it cannot actually support. RESULTS: Control passed (lam_cc=0.0 reproduces P81/P86's own min_M=0.871740335 exactly). Positive-side scan, 35 points 1e-25 to 1e8: 15/35 measured, 20/35 BLOCKED-INFRASTRUCTURE. Measurable range [0,1e-12]. Within it, min_M flat to 1.28e-11 absolute spread (0.871740335 throughout). Negative side: not run at all in the final scan, attempted separately and abandoned as BLOCKED-INFRASTRUCTURE. VERDICT rests PRIMARILY on the structural argument (true regardless of solver reach), with the numerical scan as bounded supporting evidence only, no stronger than what P86 already established.
